F.1 Why fault specifications belong in the thesis
A safety manuscript should not treat faults as vague stress labels. If a controller is evaluated
under dropout, stale values, drift, or packet disorder, the reader should know what those
words mean operationally. The purpose of this appendix is therefore to make the battery
fault surface explicit enough for reasoning and replication without drowning the reader in
repository internals.

G.2. Battery microORIUS reference adapter 282
G.2 Battery microORIUS reference adapter
The battery-first manuscript benefits from including a compact reference adapter that shows
how the abstract ORIUS logic becomes a dispatch-time clipping rule. The point of this listing
is not software novelty by itself; it is to make the framework legible to a reader who wants to
see the exact battery-domain control contract in one page.
Listing G.1: Fifteen-line battery-domainmicroORIUS adapter.
def m i c r o o r i u s _ b a t t e r y _ a d a p t e r ( action_star , soc_obs , bounds , quality ,
pmax , soc_min , soc_max ):
lo , hi = i n f l a t e _ b o u n d s ( bounds , quality )
soc_lo = max ( soc_min , soc_obs - hi )
soc_hi = min ( soc_max , soc_obs + hi )
a_clip = min ( max ( action_star , - pmax ) , pmax )
if a_clip > 0 and soc_lo <= soc_min :
a_clip = 0.0
if a_clip < 0 and soc_hi >= soc_max :
a_clip = 0.0
return a_clip
This listing is deliberately compact. It shows the essence of the battery adapter in one view:
widen uncertainty when quality falls, construct a pessimistic SOC interval, clip candidate
dispatch to battery limits, and zero actions that would continue unsafe charge or discharge
near the true-state boundary.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 317 ===
Appendix H
Four-Reviewer Critical Audit
This appendix records a four-lens critique of the battery-first manuscript. The aim is not
to weaken the thesis but to stress-test it the way strong committee members or external
reviewers often do.
H.1 Reviewer A: control-theory reviewer
What this reviewer respects.The thesis separates true and observed state cleanly, states
assumptions explicitly, and avoids pretending that one-step safety implies global stability.
The theorem block is disciplined and battery-grounded.
What this reviewer still asks for.Stronger linkage between theorem objects and executed
experiments, clearer statement of what the Core Bound is and is not, and tighter distinction
between marginal coverage statements and controller guarantees.
Blocking issue before final submission.The controlled-fault results and the subgroup-
calibration chapter must visibly map back to the assumptions used in the theorem layer.
H.2 Reviewer B: energy-systems and market reviewer
What this reviewer respects. The battery problem is real, the dispatch objective is
meaningful, and the German direct-gain surface is strong. The manuscript also honestly
states that direct economic gains depend on region structure.
What this reviewer still asks for.Per-balancing-authority decomposition, real-price
283

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 318 ===
H.3. Reviewer C: systems and runtime reviewer 284
grounding rather than proxy-only price interpretation, and a stronger explanation of why
peak shaving is zero across the US balancing authorities.
Blocking issue before final submission.The economic chapter must stop compressing
the US story into one row and must clearly state the price-data limitation.
H.3 Reviewer C: systems and runtime reviewer
What this reviewer respects.The architecture is concrete, the certificate and audit logic
are explicit, and the system is presented as a real runtime stack rather than only as theorem
prose.
What this reviewer still asks for.Component-level latency data, memory and audit
overhead, and a clearer argument that the shield can run comfortably within the control
cadence.
Blocking issue before final submission.A runtime micro-benchmark table and a short
systems-footprint section are required.
H.4 Reviewer D: experimental and validation reviewer
What this reviewer respects. The software benchmark is governed, dual-trajectory
evaluation is strong, and the hidden failure is measurable rather than rhetorical.
What this reviewer still asks for.Controlled packet-drop/jitter tables, a forty-eight-hour
fault trace in the main body, and a real HIL validation chapter to address the present
software-only limitation.
Blocking issue before final submission.Hardware-in-the-loop validation remains the
single most visible experimental gap.
H.5 Audit synthesis
All four reviewer lenses converge on the same conclusion. The battery thesis already has a
strong core claim, but it needs a last layer of experimental and systems hardening to look
fully manuscript-ready. That hardening is exactly what the new extended-validation chapters
are designed to provide.

I.2 Safety checklist
The following conditions should be satisfied before a run is considered valid:
1. independent hardware cutoff verified,
2. current and voltage limits configured below the protected-plant rating,
3. serial timestamps and logging path verified,
4. fault injector tested without live charging/discharging,
5. relay or actuator state changes manually verified,
6. emergency stop path documented and tested.

I.3. What this appendix is for 287
I.3 What this appendix is for
This appendix is deliberately operational. It exists so that the HIL chapter does not remain a
conceptual aspiration. It turns the HIL requirement into an executable experimental package
with explicit safety expectations.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 322 ===
Appendix J
Sensitivity, Latency, andSubgroup-Analysis
Protocols
This appendix records the experiment-design protocols for three missing manuscript-hardening
layers: hyperparameter sweeps, runtime latency profiling, and subgroup calibration audits.
J.1 Hyperparameter sweep protocol
The primary sweep grid should vary base miscoverageαover [0.01, 0.20] and reliability-
inflation coefficientkquality over [0.0, 1.0]. Secondary sweeps should varyinfl max, τshrink, and
λdelay. Each sweep point should record TSVR, P95 severity, IR, cost delta, and certificate
event count.
J.2 Latency profiling protocol
Every runtime stage should be timed independently: ingest, quality scoring, inflation, solve,
repair, and audit write. The reporting contract should include mean, median, p95, p99,
and max latency, together with platform information and whether the run is cold-start or
steady-state.
288

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 323 ===
J.3. Subgroup calibration protocol 289
J.3 Subgroup calibration protocol
The subgroup audit should be computed in two passes:
1. reliability- or volatility-conditioned analysis,
2. fault-family-conditioned analysis.
For each subgroup, record empirical coverage, interval width, intervention rate, and the
strongest observed calibration weakness.
J.4 Why these protocols matter
These protocols turn missing-work statements into governed evidence contracts. They make
the final hardening work reproducible and prevent the last manuscript stage from devolving
into unstructured exploratory reruns.

K.3. Coverage interpretation 294
K.3 Coverage interpretation
The matrix makes one principle visible: the manuscript is strongest where the evidence is
battery-grounded and weakest only where future locked experimental runs are still required.
This is the correct and defensible structure for a final-year battery-first manuscript. Rather
than pretending that all future validation has already been executed, the manuscript records
execution-ready chapters and tables for the remaining hardening work while keeping the core
theorem and battery evidence fully explicit.
K.4 Checklist of items requiring final locked runs
The following items are structurally integrated into the manuscript but still require final
locked experimental tables or physical execution:
1. final blackout half-life tables,
2. final graceful-landing 48-hour traces,
3. final benchmark leaderboard values under standardized fault suites,
4. final two-battery fleet composition simulation,
5. final active-probing adversarial validation,
6. final HIL logs and latency charts,
7. final hyperparameter heatmaps and runtime micro-benchmark tables.
Those items do not weaken the battery theorem core. They define the last empirical hardening
steps required to convert the monograph from a strong manuscript draft into a fully locked
final battery thesis.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 344 ===
Appendix N
Defense-Lock Replacement Templates
for Battery Hardening Chapters
This appendix is written as a practical bridge between the current manuscript draft and a
defense-ready locked battery thesis. The chapters in the late battery-hardening block are
conceptually correct and already positioned appropriately in the manuscript, but several of
them still contain drafting values, protocol tables, or execution-ready placeholders rather
than the final outputs of locked battery runs. The purpose of this appendix is to make the
replacement path explicit so the manuscript can be hardened without restructuring its core
argument.
The guiding principle is simple. The battery-first manuscript should be judged on the strength
of the battery evidence, not on how many placeholders it contains. Therefore each advanced
chapter should preserve its theoretical framing, notation, figure layout, and table schema
now, while the numerical contents of those tables and figures are replaced with the author’s
final run outputs as they become available. This appendix records exactly what those final
artifacts should look like.
N.1 How to use these templates
Each section below gives a defense-lock template for one late battery chapter. The template
has four parts: the objective of the chapter, the final table or figure set that should replace any
drafting placeholders, the minimum metadata that should accompany the replacement, and
the interpretation rule that should govern the final prose. The interpretation rule is especially
310

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 345 ===
N.2. Chapter 25: Fault-performance stress tests 311
important in a manuscript, because a final committee draft should avoid overclaiming from a
single battery run while also avoiding underselling the significance of the result.
N.2 Chapter 25: Fault-performance stress tests
The final battery stress-test chapter should report a unified fault-performance table across
the six ORIUS battery fault families. At minimum, the final replacement should include a
table with controller rows and fault-family columns, or a fault-family longtable with one row
per controller. The recommended metrics are true-state violation rate, ninety-fifth percentile
severity, intervention rate, cost delta, and useful work preserved.
Table N.1: Defense-lock template for the final battery fault-performance table. Replace all
cells with locked run outputs and cite the run manifest in the caption or footnote.
Fault Controller TSVR P95 severity IR Cost delta Useful work
Dropout Deterministic LP replace replace – replace replace
Dropout Robust LP replace replace – replace replace
Dropout CVaR / risk-aware replace replace – replace replace
Dropout DC3S replace replace replace replace replace
Delay-jitter Deterministic LP replace replace – replace replace
Delay-jitter Robust LP replace replace – replace replace
Delay-jitter CVaR / risk-aware replace replace – replace replace
Delay-jitter DC3S replace replace replace replace replace
Spike All controllers repeat for each controller

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 352 ===
Appendix O
Battery-Only Claim Discipline and Ci-
tation Policy
This appendix records the writing policy used to keep the manuscript at an appropriate
research level. A final-form monograph should be ambitious, but it should not sound inflated.
In the present manuscript, the correct ambition is abattery-domain reference monograph
that also serves as a plausible foundation for a broader ORIUS framework. The incorrect
ambition would be to describe the current battery evidence as though it already constituted
a universally validated, hardware-complete, multi-domain, production-hardened system.
O.1 Battery-only main claim
The strongest defensible main claim is the following:
The manuscript establishes a battery-domain theory-and-systems framework for
safe control under degraded observation, demonstrates a measurable gap between
observed-state and true-state safety, and shows that a quality-aware runtime shield
can close that gap within the battery evidence surface used in the thesis.
This statement is strong enough for a long-form monograph because it combines measurement,
control, runtime architecture, and theorem work. It is also narrow enough to be defended
honestly from the current evidence.
318

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 353 ===
O.2. What should remain future work 319
O.2 What should remain future work
The following claims should remain outside the current main claim path unless the supporting
runs are truly completed and locked:
1. full hardware validation beyond the battery HIL setup,
2. field deployment,
3. complete multi-agent or fleet-wide guarantees beyond the demonstrated battery-fleet
cases,
4. completed cross-domain ORIUS-Bench,
5. full universal deployment across other autonomy domains.
These topics may appear in future-work language, roadmap material, or narrowly labeled
extension chapters, but they should not be used to make the battery evidence sound broader
than it is.
O.3 Citation policy for the main body
The main body should prefer literature that directly supports one of the following:
1. battery dispatch and BESS operation,
2. robust or stochastic control under bounded disturbances,
3. conformal prediction and reliability-aware calibration,
4. runtime assurance, safe-action filtering, and control barrier logic,
5. CPS telemetry faults, replay/spoofing analogues, and battery degradation,
6. reproducibility, benchmark design, and HIL validation specific to battery settings.
Broad references used only to suggest cross-domain future potential should be concentrated
in the roadmap, outlook, or explicitly labeled future-work sections.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 354 ===
O.4. Chapter integration rule 320
O.4 Chapter integration rule
A book-style thesis should read with natural forward motion. In this manuscript that means
each chapter should answer one battery-domain question and then pass one clearly defined
unresolved tension to the next chapter. The main chain is:
1. Why the hidden gap matters,
2. how the battery domain exposes it,
3. how DC3S closes it at runtime,
4. how the battery evidence demonstrates the closure,
5. how the theorem layer explains the closure,
6. how the hardening chapters stress the battery system further,
7. how temporal, fleet, and adversarial extensions remain battery-grounded,
8. and finally how broader universalization remains future work.
O.5 Tone rules for final prose
The final prose should follow four tone rules.
1. Prefer “the present battery evidence shows” over “ORIUS proves universally.”
2. Prefer “battery-domain extension” over “final universal theorem” when the result is
still battery-scoped.
3. Prefer explicit limitations in the same chapter where the strongest claim appears.
4. Prefer operational meaning and scope boundaries over slogans.
O.6 Why this matters at the research level
A serious manuscript is judged not only by the novelty of its idea but by the correctness of
its scope. An overclaimed manuscript can feel immature even when the core contribution is

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 355 ===
O.6. Why this matters at the research level 321
strong. A well-scoped manuscript, by contrast, can support a much larger long-term research
program precisely because it is honest about what is complete and what still remains to
be done. In that sense, scope discipline is part of the scientific contribution rather than a
cosmetic writing preference.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 356 ===
Appendix P
Purpose of the advanced hardening ad-
dendum
This addendum is written to update the main 355-page battery-only thesis book with the
strongest gap analysis that emerges after stripping away formatting and evaluating the project
at the level of its core algorithms, physical assumptions, and battery-safety value. The aim is
not to change the core identity of the manuscript. That identity is already sound: a battery
controller can be safe in observed state while unsafe in true state, and a quality-aware runtime
architecture can close that gap under degraded telemetry. What this addendum does is
harden the manuscript so that the remaining weaknesses are stated precisely, translated into
battery-only work packages, and placed into the exact chapter locations where a final-year
long-form committee would expect to see them.
The addendum is intentionally battery-exclusive. It does not try to inflate the present
evidence into a false cross-domain completion story. Hardware and non-battery domains
remain future work unless physically executed. What follows is therefore written in the style
of a mature manuscript companion: it is technical, specific, restrained in claim, and explicit
about what would transform the current strong battery thesis into a defense-lock battery
monograph.
322

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 357 ===
Appendix Q
Intrinsicworthofthebattery-onlyORIUS
project
If one ignores the formatting, the long-book packaging, and even the disciplinary labels, the
battery-only ORIUS project has high intrinsic value because it addresses a real failure mode
in capital-intensive infrastructure. Grid-scale batteries are not academic toys. They are
large energy assets whose control software directly affects asset survival, replacement cost,
dispatch value, safety, and system trust. A battery dispatch controller that continues to act
confidently during stale or deceptive telemetry can push the plant into damaging overcharge,
deep discharge, high-stress operation, or cascading protective shutdown. A runtime layer that
preserves battery survival during sensor blackout while retaining useful work until the exact
point of certificate exhaustion is therefore valuable in both technical and economic terms.
That practical value is important because it explains why the battery-only framing is already
enough for a serious manuscript. The thesis does not need an autonomous vehicle simulator
to be important. It already has a plant, a degradable observation channel, a real economic
optimization surface, a measurable hidden failure, and a runtime control layer that changes
the action actually sent to the physical asset. This is precisely the sort of system in which good
long-form research can be both mathematically interesting and operationally consequential.
323

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 358 ===
Appendix R
Four-ReviewerAssessmentoftheBattery-
Only Thesis
This chapter records a synthesis of four elite-reviewer lenses: a control theorist, an machine-
learning systems expert, a battery physicist, and a cyber-physical security engineer. The
purpose is not to simulate praise. It is to identify the exact advanced gaps that remain after
the core battery thesis is already strong.
R.1 Reviewer I: control theory and mathematical sys-
tems
R.1.1 Assessment
The battery-only ORIUS program has a conceptually strong theorem spine. The formalization
of the Observation-Action Safety Gap is structurally interesting because it distinguishes
observed feasibility from true physical consequence without demanding a full POMDP
treatment. The No Free Safety theorem is valuable because it shifts the discussion from
"can we engineer something that often works" to "is quality-ignorant safety mathematically
impossible in the relevant battery fault regime." The certificate validity horizonHt and
shrinking safe-action set then add the missing temporal layer that many applied battery
papers never reach.
324

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 359 ===
R.1. Reviewer I: control theory and mathematical systems 325
R.1.2 The dynamic-drift and Lipschitz trap
The main mathematical critique is that the current tube-growth logic often summarizes plant
drift with a single constantD. That is a reasonable battery-first simplification, but it is not
the strongest mathematically disciplined version of the story. The physical plant is governed
by a state-dependent differential or difference law˙x =f(x,u ), and the effective drift can vary
significantly across the operating envelope. Near the upper charge region or under aggressive
power draw, local dynamics can stiffen, voltage may sag faster, and the true state may move
in ways that a constant drift heuristic underestimates. If the uncertainty tube grows more
slowly than the true local reachable error, the true state can slip outside the certificate while
the math still looks cosmetically clean.
R.1.3 Gap-closure principle
The correct battery-only hardening step is to replace the constant-drift heuristic with a
state- and action-dependent envelopeD(x,u ) and to tie tube growth to a reachable Lipschitz
upper bound. In manuscript terms, this means the uncertainty-growth rule should satisfy a
condition of the form
Γ t≥sup
(x,u)∈Rt
D(x,u ),
whereRt is the battery region reachable under the current certificate and dispatch mode.
This remains a battery theorem, not a universal nonlinear-control theorem, but it closes the
most obvious mathematical loophole in the current tube story.
R.1.4 What must change in the book
A final hardened version of the battery theorem block should therefore include:
1. a battery-local Lipschitz envelope definition,
2. a proposition showing that the chosen growth rule upper-bounds reachable drift,
3. a blackout or half-life rerun underD(x,u ) rather than only constantD,
4. a limitation note explaining that the result is conservative and battery-specific.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 360 ===
R.2. Reviewer II: machine learning and calibration under non-exchangeability 326
R.2 Reviewer II: machine learning and calibration un-
der non-exchangeability
R.2.1 Assessment
The use of conformalized quantile regression together with reliability-conditioned interval
inflation is one of the most elegant parts of the battery-only ORIUS story. It avoids the
pretense that one point forecaster solves deployment shift and acknowledges that interval
reliability is what matters once a controller uses uncertainty to choose actions.
R.2.2 The stale-calibration trap
The serious machine-learning critique is that batteries age. Calibration sets do not remain
young forever. If a Year-1 battery calibration surface is reused for a Year-2 battery whose
internal resistance and effective capacity have shifted, then the nominalα= 0.10 guarantee
becomes stale. This does not mean conformal prediction is wrong. It means the deployment
regime has drifted enough that static conformity memory is no longer appropriate.
R.2.3 Gap-closure principle
The natural battery-only hardening step is an online calibration maintenance loop. The
simplest first implementation is an exponentially weighted moving average of conformity
scores,
qEWMA
t = (1−λ)qEWMA
t−1 +λst,
where st is the current conformity score andλcontrols how quickly old battery behavior is
forgotten. The long-form value of this addition is not that it solves all non-exchangeability.
It is that it prevents the uncertainty layer from remaining blindly loyal to a battery regime
that no longer exists.
R.2.4 What must change in the book
The forecasting and subgroup chapters should therefore include:
1. an explicit stale-calibration limitation statement,

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 361 ===
R.3. Reviewer III: battery physics and power-systems realism 327
2. a battery-aging-aware online recalibration rule,
3. a reliability-bin table before and after the update rule,
4. a discussion of how much width is gained or lost by recalibration maintenance.
R.3 Reviewer III: battery physics and power-systems
realism
R.3.1 Assessment
The battery-only ORIUS thesis is strong from a grid-operations perspective because the
graceful-degradation logic is already operationally meaningful. It answers a real control
question: when a battery goes blind, should it continue profit-seeking dispatch, shut down
instantly, or use its remaining trusted steps to land safely? That is a much stronger
contribution than a generic warning about sensor noise.
R.3.2 The SOC-illusion trap
The physical critique is that SOC is a control abstraction, not a complete physical state.
Real batteries operate on voltage, current, thermal stress, and nonlinear electrochemical
response. A formulation that treatsC = [10%, 90%] as a flat hard wall is a useful first safety
approximation, but it does not fully capture voltage sag near the boundaries or high-stress
operation under large charge/discharge rates. If the thesis wants to look fully mature, it
should acknowledge this explicitly and propose a physically stronger simulator layer.
R.3.3 Gap-closure principle
The correct response is not to discard the current SOC-first proof surface. It is to preserve
the hard SOC wall as the current provable safety layer while adding a soft electrochemical
stress boundary. This can be done through an equivalent-circuit model and a soft stress
penalty,
ϕ(xt,a t) =λvψV (Vt) +λiψI(It) +λTψT (Tt),
which grows near damaging operating regions. This closes the gap between battery-safe
control and electrochemically conservative control without pretending that the manuscript

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 362 ===
R.4. Reviewer IV: cyber-physical security and anti-spoofing 328
already has a full chemistry-level digital twin.
R.3.4 What must change in the book
The battery dynamics and aging chapters should therefore contain:
1. a distinction between hard SOC walls and soft stress boundaries,
2. an equivalent-circuit-model extension path,
3. an asset-preservation table translating hidden violations into avoided stress or deprecia-
tion,
4. explicit wording that the current simulator is conservative and useful, but not yet the
last word in electrochemical realism.
R.4 ReviewerIV:cyber-physicalsecurityandanti-spoofing
R.4.1 Assessment
The active sensitivity probing concept is arguably one of the most original battery-only
extensions in the thesis. It shifts the security story from passive anomaly detection to
active physical challenge-response. That is a meaningful elevation from control theory into
cyber-physical security.
R.4.2 The signal-to-noise-ratio trap
The security critique is that an unstructured micro-probe may be drowned out by sensor
noise and grid jitter, especially relative to the scale of a large battery asset. A 1 MW probe
can be operationally tiny in a very large asset. If the controller cannot reliably separate the
intended physical echo from ordinary noise, then active probing becomes either ineffective or
prone to false alarms.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 363 ===
R.4. Reviewer IV: cyber-physical security and anti-spoofing 329
R.4.3 Gap-closure principle
The correct battery-only response is to move from raw random probing to structured probing.
A sinusoidal challenge at a protected frequency,
ϵt =Aprobe sin(2πfprobet +φ),
combined with a band-pass or observer/Kalman-style echo-isolation filter, creates a much
more credible battery security mechanism. The battery-only claim then becomes: ORIUS
can actively interrogate the honesty of the sensing path using bounded physical challenges
whose echoes are isolated from noise.
R.4.4 What must change in the book
The adversarial-robustness chapter should therefore include:
1. a replay or spoofing threat model,
2. a structured probe law,
3. a band-pass or observer-based echo-isolation step,
4. a detector override law that collapses trust if the echo disappears,
5. and a battery-only caution that this is a proof-of-principle defense rather than a
complete production-security theorem.

Appendix S
Mathematicalhardening: state-dependent
drift and Lipschitz-aware tube growth
S.1 Why this chapter matters
The strongest mathematical refinement identified by the reviewer panel is the move from
constant drift to state-dependent drift. This chapter spells out how that refinement should
be written in a long-form battery thesis.
S.2 Battery-domain local drift model
Let the battery state evolve according to a local nonlinear mapxt+1 =f(xt,u t) and let ˆf
denote the nominal predictor used inside the runtime. Define the local model mismatch and
reachable error by
et+1 =f(xt,u t)−ˆf(ˆxt, ˆut).
A conservative battery-only bound is
∥et+1∥≤Lx(xt,u t)∥et∥+Lu(xt,u t)∥ut−ˆut∥+δf,
where Lx and Lu are local Lipschitz envelopes andδf is residual model mismatch.
331

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 366 ===
S.3. Tube-growth condition 332
S.3 Tube-growth condition
Define a tube-growth scheduleΓ t such that
Γ t≥sup
(x,u)∈Rt
(Lx(x,u )∥et∥+Lu(x,u )∥ut−ˆut∥+δf).
Then the battery uncertainty tube is conservatively valid over one step.
Proposition S.1(Battery-local tube validity). Ifxt∈Ut and the uncertainty-growth schedule
satisfies the dominating-envelope condition above, thenxt+1∈Ut+1 under the propagated
battery tube.
Proof. The claim follows by direct substitution of the one-step reachable error bound into
the chosen tube update. Because the schedule dominates the maximal reachable one-step
deviation over the admissible region, the propagated true state remains in the enlarged set.
The proposition is conservative and local but closes the constant-drift loophole.
S.4 What final evidence should accompany the theorem
A final manuscript chapter should not stop at the proposition. It should show:
1. a heatmap of the battery-local drift envelope over SOC and power,
2. a comparison between constant-D and state-dependent tube growth,
3. updated blackout-horizon tables using the strengthened growth law.

Appendix T
Machine-learning hardening: online re-
calibration under battery aging
T.1 Aging-aware calibration maintenance
A battery is not stationary over its service life. Even if the load and weather regime were
identical, the plant itself changes. This means a fully static conformal calibration layer is a
temporary approximation rather than a complete deployment solution.
T.2 EWMA conformity maintenance
The core maintenance equation is
qEWMA
t = (1−λ)qEWMA
t−1 +λst.
Recent scores therefore outweigh older ones, allowing the interval system to remain more
faithful to the battery’s current operating regime.
333

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 368 ===
T.3. Why this is still battery-first rather than universal overclaim 334
T.3 Why this is still battery-first rather than universal
overclaim
Thethesisshouldnotclaimthatthissolvescalibrationunderallnon-exchangeableprocesses. It
should claim the narrower and stronger thing: the battery controller now contains a mechanism
for preventing Year-1 conformity memory from dominating Year-2 battery behavior.
T.4 Recommended evidence package
The chapter should ultimately report before/after subgroup coverage, width cost, long-horizon
stability, and one battery-aging simulation pass showing why the update loop matters.

Appendix U
Battery-physics hardening: soft electro-
chemical stress boundaries
U.1 The hard-wall versus soft-boundary distinction
The manuscript’s current hard SOC walls remain useful and should not be removed. But a
final battery-only monograph becomes much stronger if it says explicitly that those walls are
the inner safety approximation, not the totality of physical battery realism.
U.2 Equivalent-circuit extension
An equivalent-circuit model gives the thesis a practical path toward voltage-aware realism
without abandoning computational tractability. The battery-only claim is then that ORIUS
can be lifted from a flat SOC-safe model into a richer but still tractable ECM-safe model.
U.3 Soft stress penalty
A battery-aware dispatch objective can therefore use a penalty of the form
ϕ(xt,a t) =λvψV (Vt) +λiψI(It) +λTψT (Tt),
so that control not only avoids explicit violation but also avoids repeatedly pressing the plant
into high-stress operating zones.

Appendix V
Security hardening: structured active
probing and echo isolation
V.1 From passive anomaly detection to active battery
interrogation
A battery-only manuscript becomes significantly more novel when the runtime no longer
merely watches for missing packets or obvious spikes. Instead, it asks the sensing path to
answer a physical question. That is what active probing does.
V.2 Structured sinusoidal probe
Use a bounded sinusoidal probe
ϵt =Aprobe sin(2πfprobet +φ),
and superimpose it on the nominal dispatch command. Because the probe has a known
frequency and amplitude bound, the expected physical echo can be isolated much more
reliably than a raw random blip.
337

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 372 ===
V.3. Echo isolation via band-pass or observer filtering 338
V.3 Echo isolation via band-pass or observer filtering
Let the observed residual bert. A band-pass or observer-based filter produces a filtered
residual ˜rt concentrated around the probe dynamics. The detector then evaluates whether the
filtered residual remains consistent with the known challenge signal. If not, trust collapses.
Theorem V.1 (Battery-only active-probing detection principle). Suppose the adversary
cannot predict the protected probe sequence before actuation and the filtered echo remains
bounded away from zero under honest telemetry. Then any sustained spoofing stream that
fails to reproduce the probe-consistent echo will, in finite time, force the trust score below the
protection threshold under the detector override law.
Proof. Under honest telemetry the filtered residual retains a nontrivial correlation with
the protected probe. Under sustained spoofing that does not preserve the physical echo,
the filtered consistency statistic falls below threshold with repeated probe windows. The
override law therefore collapses trust and triggers the ordinary ORIUS protected mode. The
result is battery-specific and proof-of-principle; it does not claim full security against every
adversary.

Appendix X
Publicationlevelassessmentanduniversal-
foundation discipline
The battery-only ORIUS project is already clearly above course-project and standard master’s-
project level. It contains a measurable hidden safety failure, a runtime architecture that
changes plant-bound actions, a benchmark logic, a theorem scaffold, and a serious extension
path. That is already long-form research territory.
At the same time, the best universal sentence remains disciplined: the battery thesis provides
a strong reference-domain foundation for a broader ORIUS framework, but it is not yet
complete universal validation. That sentence should remain the governing claim throughout
the final manuscript.

Y.1 What should be locked first
The most efficient final replacement order is:
1. the battery fault-performance table,

the battery evidence where the thesis itself already says the main practical gaps remain: fault
stress testing, systems runtime budget, and hardware credibility.

Y.3 Closing statement

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 378 ===
Appendix Z
Detailed chapter-by-chapter hardening
templates for Chapters 25–36
This chapter provides a more explicit chapter-by-chapter hardening guide for the late battery-
only extension block. Each section is written to be thesis-usable: it states the purpose of
the chapter, the exact evidence object that would make the chapter defense-ready, and the
way the chapter should be framed so that it strengthens the battery-only thesis without
overstating what has already been empirically proved.
Z.1 Chapter 25: fault-performance stress tests
The correct role of the fault-performance chapter is to prove that the hidden battery safety
gap is not a one-plot curiosity. The chapter should compare controller families across the
battery fault surface and should be read as a systems chapter rather than as a theorem
chapter. The final table should include at minimum dropout, delay-jitter, spike, stale, drift,
and reorder, with rows for deterministic LP, robust LP, a risk-aware baseline, andDC3S.
The strongest version of the chapter also includes a short paragraph interpreting which fault
family is most damaging and why drift and stale behavior are structurally dangerous.

Z.2 Chapter 26: runtime latency and systems footprint
The systems chapter proves that the battery runtime is not only mathematically motivated

median, p95, p99, and max. It should also state the environment in which the benchmark was
run. The tone should remain practical: the question is not whether the code is universally
real-time for every future setting, but whether the battery runtime is comfortably inside the
loop budget of the current battery controller.

The hyperparameter chapter should be positioned as a robustness chapter, not as a treasure

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 381 ===
Z.4. Chapter 28: conditional coverage and subgroup calibration 347
Z.4 Chapter 28: conditional coverage and subgroup
calibration
This chapter should remain honest and diagnostic. It should say clearly that the manuscript
already supports marginal coverage reasoning and identifies subgroup weaknesses, but that
full conditional validity remains a stronger target. The chapter becomes much stronger when
it presents reliability-bin tables, fault-conditioned subgroup tables, and a short narrative
explaining why solar and US wind are especially difficult surfaces.
Z.5 Chapter 29: regional decomposition and real-price
grounding
The regional chapter should answer two questions. First, is the US result hiding heterogeneity
across balancing authorities? Second, are the current economic signals proxies or real prices?
The chapter becomes credible when it answers these questions directly. Even if full real-price
replacement is not finished, the chapter should state what the proxy limitation means and
what exact data replacement path is planned.
Z.6 Chapter 30: battery aging and asset preservation
This chapter should avoid pretending that the present battery thesis has already become a full
electrochemistry monograph. A better and stronger stance is to translate hidden true-state
violations into avoided stress, avoided degradation proxy, and avoided depreciation. If a
richer electrochemical or ECM-based model is later added, this chapter becomes the bridge
between runtime safety and asset economics.
Z.7 Chapter 31: HIL architecture and validation
The HIL chapter remains the most visible evidence gap in the battery-only thesis. A final
HIL chapter should contain a setup diagram, a timing decomposition table, a fault-response
figure, and a short discussion of what the HIL rig does and does not prove. The chapter
should sound like a careful systems-validation chapter, not a heroic proof-of-fire narrative.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 382 ===
Z.8. Chapters 32 and 33: blackout half-life and graceful degradation 348
Z.8 Chapters 32 and 33: blackout half-life and graceful
degradation
These two chapters work best as a pair. The first shows how trust decays under blackout;
the second shows how the battery uses its remaining trusted horizon to land safely. The
half-life chapter needs real blackout traces. The graceful-degradation chapter needs actual
mode-comparison traces. Together they create one of the strongest battery-only contributions
in the thesis.
Z.9 Chapters 34, 35, and 36: battery benchmark, com-
position, and adversarial robustness
These three chapters should remain explicitly battery-only and extension-oriented. The
benchmark chapter should avoid pretending the universal benchmark is done. The fleet
chapter should avoid implying that general multi-agent safety is solved. The adversarial
chapter should avoid implying production-grade cyber-security closure. The strength of all
three chapters comes from disciplined scope and good battery-specific examples.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 383 ===
Appendix
Natural-flow writing rules for a final-
year research monograph
A good final-year thesis is not only correct. It also reads like a coherent book written by one
careful mind rather than like a bundle of papers stapled together. This chapter records the
writing rules that matter most for the current battery-only thesis.
.1 Rule 1: move from problem to mechanism to evi-
dence
Each chapter should open by naming the battery problem that motivates it, then explain
the mechanism, then present evidence or theorems. Chapters that begin immediately with
formulas often read colder and less mature than chapters that first tell the reader why the
formula belongs there.
.2 Rule 2: use modest claims in strong chapters
A research thesis rarely benefits from grand language. The strongest writing in the current
battery manuscript already uses the right tone: the battery evidence suggests, the battery
experiments show, the present manuscript does not yet prove, the current chapter is battery-
specific. This measured style makes the work sound more serious, not less.
349

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 384 ===
.3. Rule 3: keep hardware and other domains in future work unless executed 350
.3 Rule 3: keep hardware and other domains in future
work unless executed
One of the biggest risks in late-stage manuscript editing is to import ambitious hardware
or cross-domain language into chapters whose evidence is still simulation-only. The best
battery-only thesis resists this temptation. It keeps hardware and other domains clearly
marked as future work unless those experiments have really been completed.
.4 Rule 4: pair every late extension chapter with one
visual anchor
The later battery chapters should each have one visual anchor that the committee can
remember: a fault table, a 48-hour trace, a latency table, a blackout decay plot, a landing-
mode comparison, a fleet-composition figure, or a spoofing-detection plot. Long prose without
a memorable visual anchor tends to weaken committee recall.
.5 Rule 5: preserve the battery-only hierarchy of claim
The final battery-only hierarchy should be repeated across the thesis: the battery domain
reveals the hidden gap; the runtime closes it; the theorem block supports why; the hard-
ening chapters extend the battery story in time, systems, security, and economics; broader
universalization remains future work. If this hierarchy is preserved, the book flows naturally.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 385 ===
Appendix
Committee-style questions and thesis-
ready answers
This chapter collects likely high-level committee questions and gives thesis-appropriate
answers. The point is not to script a defense performance. The point is to test whether the
manuscript itself has coherent answers.
.1 Question: Why is this not just another robust bat-
tery controller?
Because the central failure mode is not simply future uncertainty in load, solar, or price. It is
uncertainty about whether the controller’s current state representation remains trustworthy at
the moment of action. Standard robust dispatch can still be robust over the wrong observed
state. ORIUS changes the problem by conditioning safe control on observation reliability
itself.
.2 Question: Why does the hidden gap matter physi-
cally?
Because batteries are physical assets with damaging edge behavior. If the controller believes
it is at a safe SOC while the true battery is materially closer to a constraint boundary, then
the controller can keep issuing commands that are internally feasible but physically unsafe.
351

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 386 ===
.3. Question: Why is battery enough for a serious manuscript? 352
The hidden gap therefore matters both for safety and for asset health.
.3 Question: Why is battery enough for a serious
manuscript?
Because the battery domain already contains the full ORIUS pattern: a true plant, a
degradable observation channel, a meaningful action space, useful-work objectives, physical
safety boundaries, and a runtime point where safe repair matters. One strong domain is
enough when that domain supports the full intellectual structure.
.4 Question: What remains to be done before the
strongest defense version?
The most important remaining replacements are a locked fault-performance table, a flagship
48-hour dropout trace, a locked latency micro-benchmark, and a real HIL package. These
are not conceptual rescues; they are the final evidence-hardening steps.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 387 ===
Appendix
Main-body versus future-work citation
policy
This chapter records a simple editorial rule. Citations that directly strengthen the battery
thesis belong in the main body. Citations that mainly support future cross-domain general-
ization, hardware deployment outside the current evidence, or universal ambitions not yet
validated should be moved to future work, the roadmap, or an outlook appendix. This rule
prevents the main chapters from sounding broader than their evidence supports.
.1 Keep in the main body
Battery dispatch, robust and stochastic control, conformal prediction, runtime assurance,
control barrier and safety literature, CPS telemetry fault work, battery degradation literature,
replay/FDIA/watermark analogues, and battery benchmarking.
.2 Move to future work
References used mainly to motivate autonomous vehicles, surgery, aerospace, industrial
transfer, universal hardware deployment, or broad cross-domain benchmarking should remain
outside the main evidence path unless actual evidence is present.
353

Final pre-defense lock checklist
The battery-only manuscript can be considered substantially defense-ready when the following
are true:
1. the main fault-performance table is locked,
2. the flagship 48-hour trace is locked,
3. the latency micro-benchmark is locked,
4. the HIL chapter is either executed or explicitly moved to future work,
5. no main-body table uses synthetic placeholders,
6. the battery-only claim discipline is preserved throughout,
7. every theorem chapter and appendix proof cross-reference resolves correctly.
At that point, the thesis will read not merely as a long draft, but as a coherent battery-only
monograph ready for committee-level scrutiny.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 389 ===
Appendix
Purpose of the addendum
This addendum consolidates the final editorial and manuscript-hardening guidance that is
most important for a defense-ready battery-only thesis. The goal is not to change the scientific
core of the manuscript. The goal is to make the narrative more mature, less slogan-like,
and more clearly aligned with what a last-year long-form manuscript should actually sound
like. In particular, it provides two things that are often missing when a long technical
manuscript has grown iteratively: first, a statement of battery-only claim discipline that
prevents overclaiming; second, a chapter-by-chapter replacement plan for the late chapters
whose tables and figures still need to be locked to final battery outputs.
The addendum therefore assumes the battery thesis body already exists and is already strong
in its central argument: a controller can be safe in the observed state while unsafe in the true
physical battery state, and a quality-aware runtime shield can close that gap in the battery
evidence surface. What remains is to make the advanced chapters read with natural forward
flow and with the restraint expected of a mature manuscript.
355

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 390 ===
Appendix
Battery-only claim discipline
The strongest defensible claim of the manuscript is not thatORIUS is already universally
proven in every physical AI domain. The strongest defensible claim is that the battery
domain now provides a reference implementation and a reference evidence surface from which
a broader framework can be lifted later. This distinction matters because long-form work is
judged not only by novelty but by the precision of its scope. A manuscript that overclaims
universality too early often looks less mature than one that states a narrower claim clearly
and proves it well.
Accordingly, the battery thesis should use language of the following kind in its strongest
chapters: the present battery evidence shows, the battery-domain experiments demonstrate,
the battery runtime indicates, the battery theorem layer supports, and the battery benchmark
track measures. Broader cross-domain language should be reserved for outlook and future
work. The writing should therefore distinguish among three layers of claim. The first layer is
proved now in battery. The second layer is structurally suggestive of a broader framework.
The third layer is future work that the current battery results make plausible but do not yet
validate. Keeping those three layers distinct improves the tone and credibility of the entire
monograph.
.1 What belongs in the main body
The main body should remain centered on battery-core literature and battery-core evidence:
battery dispatch under uncertainty, robust and stochastic control, conformal calibration,
runtime assurance, battery degradation, CPS telemetry faults, battery security analogues,
and battery benchmarking. References that mainly justify transfer to autonomous driving,
356

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 391 ===
.2. How to phrase the universal ambition 357
surgery, aerospace, or generic universal deployment should not sit in the middle of the main
evidence chapters. They can appear in the roadmap or future-work framing, but they should
not be used to make the battery evidence sound broader than it is.
.2 How to phrase the universal ambition
The correct manuscript sentence is that the current battery work provides a strong foundation
for a broader universal framework. The incorrect sentence is that full universal validation
has already been completed. Battery-only depth is not a weakness here; it is the source of
the thesis’s coherence.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 392 ===
Appendix
Natural-flow revisions for the late ad-
vanced chapters
.1 Certificate half-life under blackout
The certificate half-life chapter should be written as the first natural temporal extension of the
battery theorem block. It should begin by reminding the reader that earlier chapters prove
local battery safety under bounded observation degradation, but do not yet tell the operator
how long those guarantees remain usable when the SCADA stream becomes stale. This
chapter then answers that temporal question. The chapter should sound like an operations
chapter with theorem support, not like an abstract theory digression.
A useful chapter rhythm is: battery blackout scenario, margin definition, drift bound, validity
horizon, half-life interpretation, blackout cases, and limits. The concluding paragraph should
say that the chapter does not solve universal certificate theory for every domain. It solves
the battery-domain question of how trust decays under blackout and when battery control
must become more conservative.
.2 Graceful degradation and safe landing
The graceful degradation chapter should read as the control-policy consequence of the previous
half-life chapter. Once the remaining certificate horizon is finite, the controller must decide
what to do with the remaining trusted steps. The correct narrative sequence is therefore
not abstract theorem first, but battery control problem first: the battery cannot continue
358

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 393 ===
.3. Battery benchmark track 359
arbitrage blindly, but it also need not drop offline instantly if the remaining horizon is still
nonzero. This chapter should make the safe-landing idea feel operational and intuitive.
A mature version of the chapter should avoid heroic language such as “the battery saves
itself”. A better style is to speak about battery landing policy, neutral reserve region, useful
work preserved, and controlled tapering into fallback. The prose should sound like a careful
systems manuscript, not a pitch deck.
.3 Battery benchmark track
The benchmark chapter should sound like a measurement-standard chapter rather than
a universal benchmark manifesto. The battery-only benchmark is already important. It
gives the field a repeatable battery fault surface, explicit true-vs-observed evaluation, and
a controller comparison protocol. The chapter should therefore emphasize what is already
complete in battery and make the broader benchmark vision explicit but secondary.
.4 Battery-fleet composition
The composition chapter should sound like a battery-fleet extension, not a finished general
multi-agent safety theorem. The cleanest prose is to show that one battery can become
locally conservative under degraded trust while another battery shares the same transformer
constraint, and therefore local safety caps do not automatically compose. This gives the
chapter a concrete operational problem instead of a vague universal ambition.
.5 Adversarial robustness and active probing
The security chapter should be written as a battery-domain cyber-physical security extension.
Its strongest contribution is not to claim complete protection against all advanced persistent
threats. Its strongest contribution is to show that if passive telemetry-health indicators
can be spoofed, then the battery controller can actively interrogate the sensing path with
a bounded physical challenge-response mechanism. This chapter should therefore remain
framed as a proof-of-principle battery defense.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 394 ===
Appendix
Defense-lock replacement templates for
the late battery chapters
The late chapters are conceptually strong, but several of them still function partly as execution-
ready chapters: they have the right theory, the right table layouts, and the right figure logic,
but they still need the author’s final battery runs to replace drafting placeholders. This
chapter records what the final replacement package should look like.
.1 Fault-performance table
The final table should report deterministic, robust, CVaR or risk-aware, and DC3S controllers
across the battery fault family: dropout, delay-jitter, spike, stale, drift, and reorder. The
minimum metrics are true-state violation rate, ninety-fifth percentile severity, intervention
rate, cost delta, and useful work preserved. A final defense version should not rely only on
one aggregate hidden-gap number. It should show the fault surface explicitly.

The final trace should be a multi-panel figure showing at minimum observed SOC, true SOC,

fault-active shading. This figure should be able to tell the manuscript’s entire battery story in
one page: fault begins, truth and observation diverge, the quality score collapses, uncertainty
widens, the shield intervenes, and the physical battery remains safe.
.3 Latency micro-benchmark
The final systems chapter should report component-level runtime and full loop runtime in
milliseconds, with mean, median, p95, p99, and maximum. The environment metadata should
be recorded in the chapter or its table note.

The advanced battery chapters on regional decomposition, asset preservation, battery-fleet

all obey the same rule: final battery runs replace synthetic tables, and the writing remains
battery-specific unless there is truly broader evidence.

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 397 ===
Appendix
Priority-ranked final lock checklist
Before the manuscript is frozen for a defense version, the following replacements should be
completed in roughly this order.
1. Final battery fault-performance table under packet drop, jitter, and the remaining
battery fault families.
2. Final 48-hour dropout or stale-trace figure with observed/true SOC, reliability score,
interval width, and actions.
3. Final runtime latency micro-benchmark table.
4. Final HIL setup diagram, timing table, and at least one real fault-response trace.
5. Final reliability-bin or subgroup calibration outputs.
6. Final battery blackout runs for certificate half-life and graceful degradation.
7. Final battery-fleet composition simulations.
8. Final active-probing spoofing simulations.
If the first four items are completed, the thesis will feel much closer to a true defense-lock
manuscript even if some of the later exploratory battery extensions remain battery-only
protocol chapters.
363

=== PHYSICAL PAGE 398 ===
Appendix
Closing statement
The current battery-only manuscript is already strong in its core. It identifies a real hidden
safety failure, measures it on a battery system, introduces a runtime control architecture
that closes it, and builds a theorem backbone around that closure. The remaining work
is therefore not about inventing a new thesis. It is about hardening the strongest one you
already have. That hardening is best achieved by keeping the main claim battery-specific, by
replacing synthetic late-chapter tables with locked battery outputs, and by allowing hardware
and non-battery domains to remain future work unless they are truly executed.
